**Structured Initial Assessment of the current state of the Transport Capacity Balance Identity and what an explicit constructive allocation algorithm would actually need to compute from the pole-pair and trivial-zero atoms**

The Transport Capacity Balance Identity remains the primary documented obstruction for turning the candidate proportional allocation rule into an exact construction for the packetwise measures η_{p,q,z} (observed from the read strategy, allocation rule, and identity documents; remains open for the infinite trivial-zero reservoir).

**Candidate construction under test on this finite regime:** The identity is the sidewise equality T_-(z) = D_+(z), T_+(z) = D_-(z) for z > 0 in the common folded-kernel domain.

- D_{p,q}(z) is the odd drift of a chamber packet, aggregated to D±(z) over sign-split packets. These derive from PGS objects and invariants (deconvolved chamber-load λ = Λ(n) after divisor-count field and GWR processing, folded in the centered coordinate per the Chamber-Deconvolved Reciprocal Balance Lemma).

- The transport reservoir atoms are the pole-pair and trivial-zero atoms (y_j, a_j(z)) with centered radius |y_j| >= 1/2.

- Odd capacity of each such atom: O_j(z) = a_j(z) J_z(y_j).

- T±(z) the signed aggregates of these O_j over the reservoir atoms.

The required conditions for the identity (candidate) include global odd balance (sum D + sum O = 0), the sidewise match, zero-radius neutrality (J_z(0) = 0, placing gamma regularization and main constants outside the odd capacity budget), and convergent exchange of sums.

The global completion negative cost conditions require the packetwise η (negative parts localized) plus zero-radius nonnegative background to assemble exactly to the classical C_comp(s) = -1/s -1/(s-1) + (1/2)log π - (1/2)(Γ'/Γ)(s/2), satisfying the four theorem conditions with no unassigned negative folded cost (using K_z for the cost integrals).

**What an explicit constructive allocation algorithm would actually need to compute from the pole-pair and trivial-zero atoms (classical analysis of completion terms only):**

From the pole-pair atoms (explicit contributions in C_comp) and trivial-zero transport atoms (|y_j| >= 1/2 in the Gamma/completion expression):

- Centered positions y_j and z-dependent masses a_j(z) for each atom (from the explicit decomposition of C_comp(s)).

- The folded kernel J_z evaluated at the y_j.

- Signed odd capacities O_j(z) = a_j(z) J_z(y_j) for each.

- Aggregated T+(z) and T-(z) by signing and summing over the reservoir atoms.

These classical quantities feed the proportional opposite-sign allocation rule (candidate construction from the allocation rule document): opposite-sign atoms are distributed to packets proportionally to their drift demand (θ = D_p,q / D± for the relevant side). The resulting packetwise η_{p,q,z} is the sum of allocated atom shares. This preserves exact assembly (θ sum to 1 per atom), localization (by radius >=1/2 > M_p,q), and (subject to the identity or background assignment) the drift match and controlled summation.

On finite chambers under test on this finite regime, the algorithm computes the above O_j and T± from the classical atoms (pole pair explicit/finite; trivial zeros truncated), pairs with PGS-derived D_p,q (from PGS objects only), applies the rule, and checks the four theorem conditions numerically.

Gamma regularization is assigned to the zero-radius nonnegative reservoir (not transport negative). The infinite summation over the trivial-zero reservoir and full convergence of the allocation in K_z remain open.

**Strategy note:** The classical computation of O_j(z) and T±(z) from the atoms provides the reservoir capacities for the candidate allocation. It does not select or determine the PGS packet drifts D or the packet definitions; those are derived from PGS objects and invariants. The sidewise identity is the open target.

**Restatement of the Exact Completion Assembly construction obligation in pure PGS-native terms (re-expressed from PGS objects and invariants; never from C_comp(s)):**

The ordered prime-gap state is partitioned into chambers delimited by consecutive prime endpoints p < q. Each chamber contains the packet P(p,q) of structural points n with associated local centered coordinates x_n. The divisor-count field τ(n) on the packet points, together with the Leftmost Minimum-Divisor Rule (GWR), selects the ordered structure and the zero-excess coordinate inside the packet. Dirichlet deconvolution of the chamber-load H(n) = (τ(n) log n)/2 by the divisor-count series produces the deconvolved coefficients λ(n) on the packet. The packet drift is the PGS object D_{p,q}(z) = ∑_{n∈P(p,q)} λ(n) J_z(x_n) in the folded kernel at scale z. The packet excursion radius is the PGS object M_{p,q} = max |x_n| over the packet (satisfying M_{p,q} < 1/2).

A chamber/packet carries unresolved PGS state when its drift D_{p,q}(z) is nonzero and unmatched. The Exact Completion Assembly requires an explicit construction of the packetwise measures η_{p,q,z} (via deterministic allocation weights θ_{p,q,j}(z) or direct definition from the packet's GWR-ordered divisor-count field, its M_{p,q}, and its deconvolved λ field) such that:

- the negative part satisfies the localization invariant supp(η^−_{p,q,z}) ⊆ {|x| ≥ M_{p,q}} (outside the packet excursion);
- zero-radius constants contribute only to the global nonnegative background (no leakage into any η^−);
- the assembled sum over packets plus background exactly matches the required target on the tested domain (exact assembly);
- the negative folded costs remain summable in the kernel (controlled summation).

The candidate explicit algorithm realizing the η_{p,q,z} is the proportional opposite-sign allocation rule from the transport reservoir (atoms of centered radius ≥ 1/2, i.e., pole-pair and trivial-zero atoms whose odd capacities O_j(z) = a_j(z) J_z(y_j) are split into T+ and T−). Packets with positive drift D receive negative odd correction from the T− reservoir in proportion to demand; packets with negative drift receive the opposite. This rule is fully deterministic and uses only PGS packet objects. It achieves int J_z dη_{p,q,z} = −D_{p,q}(z) exactly when the Transport Capacity Balance Identity T−(z) = D+(z) and T+(z) = D−(z) holds.

The verification surface on finite chambers is the direct, deterministic computation on any chosen finite set of chambers: enumerate the chambers; compute all PGS objects explicitly (endpoints p,q; packet points and x_n; divisor-count field τ; GWR-selected positions and zero-excess coordinate; exact prefix deconvolution yielding λ(n) per packet; resulting D_{p,q}(z) and M_{p,q} at chosen z); apply the candidate allocation rule to produce the η corrections; numerically verify the four conditions (localization of negative support, absence of zero-radius leakage in packet negative parts, exact assembly of the finite sum against the target, and drift cancellation). Any mismatch on a tested chamber invalidates the candidate algorithm or isolates the precise finite algebraic structure that a symbolic proof must explain. Success on large finite regimes constitutes the required verification surface while the Transport Capacity Balance Identity and the infinite trivial-zero reservoir summation remain fully open (per the session success criterion "constructive algorithm + verification surface" with explicit open bounds).

All derivation and critique begins strictly from the PGS objects (ordered prime-gap state, chambers, packets P(p,q), GWR, divisor-count field, M_{p,q}, deconvolved λ(n), D_{p,q}(z), zero-excess coordinate) and PGS invariants (Divisor Normalization Identity realized by the deconvolution, localization by packet excursion M versus reservoir radius 1/2, reciprocal transport in the folded kernel, GWR ordering) and applies PGS rules/laws (GWR interior maximizer for packet order, proportional allocation, zero-radius nonnegative assignment, support condition) to yield resolved, unresolved, or invalidated PGS states on the finite chambers under test. The classical target formula appears only as the exact-match verification condition on the output of the PGS-native assembly, never as starting point or inference mechanism.

### 2026-05-25 — NUMERICS subagent initial contribution
**Subagent identity:** NUMERICS (verification surface and experimental harness design). Subagent ID: `019e5d84-32e4-74a1-b79e-c72a721a6cdc` (from TEAM-COORDINATION-2026-05-25-01.md).

**Status:** Candidate verification surface and minimal viable harness plan recorded. No construction of the packetwise measures η_{p,q,z} has been completed or claimed. All proposals, code sketches, and checks are restricted to finite chamber sets. The Transport Capacity Balance Identity and infinite trivial-zero reservoir summation remain fully open.

**Role-specific assessment of current obstruction (from Numerics perspective, PGS packet objects first):** 
The Exact Completion Assembly Theorem requires an explicit packetwise construction of η_{p,q,z} from the transport reservoir (pole pair + trivial-zero atoms) that satisfies the four conditions while exactly cancelling each packet's odd drift D_{p,q}(z). The candidate route is the Transport Reservoir Allocation Rule (proportional opposite-sign allocation of reservoir atoms to packets by drift demand). The primary documented obstruction is the Transport Capacity Balance Identity (T_-(z) = D_+(z) and T_+(z) = D_-(z)) for the complete infinite reservoir. From the verification surface, this manifests as: any finite truncation of packets and sampled reservoir atoms will exhibit a measurable imbalance |T_\pm - D_\mp|; localization and non-negativity at zero radius hold by construction of the rule (transport atoms have |y_j| ≥ 1/2 while packet M_{p,q} < 1/2), but exact global assembly and controlled infinite summation cannot be verified on any finite regime.

**Reusable scripts and tools identified (exact paths, for extension in harness):**
- `research/12-rh-bridge/tools/kernel_packet_diagnostic.py`: Primary reusable artifact. Implements deterministic generation of all consecutive-prime (p, q) packets for q ≤ LIMIT=1_000_000, extracts P(p,q) = {q} ∪ interior prime powers with λ(n) and centered x_n = log(n) - 0.5(log p + log q), computes M_{p,q} = max |x_n|, D_{p,q}(z) and R_{p,q}(z) for a grid of z values using the folded kernels K_z(x) = 1/(z + x²), J_z(x) = x K_z(x). Directly extensible for the full harness (packet generation + precompute of demands). 
- `src/python/z_band_prime_rh_bridge/bridge.py`: Provides `mangoldt_values_up_to(limit)` and `divisor_counts_up_to(limit)` (via smallest-prime-factor table) for efficient precomputation of λ tables and related coefficients. Reusable for exact λ on prime powers without duplication.
- `research/01-generator/scripts/prime_inference_generator/` (e.g., `experimental_pgs_prime_emitter.py`, `pure_generator.py` and submodules, `boundary_certificate_graph_solver.py`): PGS-native chamber and prime-endpoint generation machinery. Can supply ordered lists of (p, q) endpoints and GWR selectors for packet enumeration at scale.
- `research/02-gwr-dni/scripts/` (e.g., `gwr_recursive_gap_walk.py`, `gwr_dni_recursive_walk.py`, and `proof/` subdirectory scans such as `large_gap_margin_scan.py`, `prime_gap_admissibility_frontier.py`): GWR/DNI-ordered chamber traversal and large-regime gap scans. Suitable for generating real (p, q) sequences with associated divisor data for packet construction.
- `src/c/high-scale-pgs/src/pgs_chamber.c` (and `pgs_emit.c`, `include/pgs_high_scale.h`, build artifacts): High-scale C implementation of integer-level chamber logic. Available for regimes beyond convenient Python iteration (e.g., primes to 10^7+), with existing test_integer_chamber.c for validation.
- `research/12-rh-bridge/docs/` (supporting definitions): `exact_completion_assembly_theorem.md`, `transport_reservoir_allocation_rule.md`, `transport_capacity_balance_identity.md`, `folded_packet_drift_inequality.md`, `packet_drift_weighted_average_lemma.md`, `exact_completion_assembly_strategy.md`, `kernel_weighted_prime_power_packet_diagnostic.md` (the generated output of the diagnostic above). These supply the exact packet definition P(p,q), the four conditions, D_{p,q}(z), M_{p,q}, kernels, and localization statements.

**Concrete plan for minimal viable verification harness (MVH):**
The MVH will be implemented as a new deterministic script under the experiments directory per the charter: `research/12-rh-bridge/proof-construction/experiments/verify_candidate_eta_allocation.py` (plus supporting library factors extracted from the diagnostic). All code will begin from PGS packet objects (ordered prime-gap chambers, prime-power packets P(p,q), λ-weighted measures, GWR selectors) before any completion atoms. No classical primality or probabilistic methods inside the verification loops. Output will be a report .md (and optional .jsonl) carrying strict status headers on every section.

- **Packet generation for large numbers of real (p, q) packets with M_{p,q} and D_{p,q}(z):** Extend the exact logic in `kernel_packet_diagnostic.py`. Precompute smallest-prime-factor or divisor table + Mangoldt λ up to LIMIT via the bridge.py helpers. Iterate consecutive primes p < q ≤ LIMIT (skip gaps of 1). For each chamber: compute center_log; collect packet as endpoint q (λ = log q, x_q) plus every interior n = r^a (a≥2) with λ = log r and x_n; compute M_{p,q} = max |x_n| over the packet. For each z in a chosen grid, compute exactly D_{p,q}(z) = ∑ λ(n) J_z(x_n) and R_{p,q}(z) = ∑ λ(n) K_z(x_n). This produces the required input (M, D(z) for multiple z) for thousands to hundreds of thousands of packets depending on LIMIT. Store per-packet records for downstream allocation.

- **First "candidate allocation algorithm" sketch (proportional opposite-sign rule, ready for direct testing):** The following implements exactly the rule stated in `transport_reservoir_allocation_rule.md`. It takes precomputed packets (with D(z)) and a reservoir model (list of transport atoms (y_j, a_j(z)) with |y_j| ≥ 1/2 supplied by Analyst or stubbed from explicit C_comp terms + sampled trivial zeros). It produces per-packet shares θ and the resulting packet measures η_{p,q,z} as weighted sums of the allocated atoms.

  ```python
  # Candidate (first testable version) — strict separation: this is a candidate algorithm under test on finite regime only.
  def compute_drift_demands(packets, z):
      D_pos = 0.0
      D_neg = 0.0
      pos_list = []
      neg_list = []
      for pkt in packets:
          d = pkt["D"][z]
          if d > 0:
              D_pos += d
              pos_list.append(pkt)
          elif d < 0:
              D_neg += (-d)
              neg_list.append(pkt)
      return D_pos, D_neg, pos_list, neg_list

  def build_transport_reservoir(z, pole_pair_atoms, trivial_zero_sample):
      # pole_pair_atoms and trivial_zero_sample provided externally (explicit formulas)
      atoms = []
      for y, a in (pole_pair_atoms + trivial_zero_sample):
          if abs(y) >= 0.5:  # transport reservoir only
              J = y / (z + y * y) if (z + y*y) != 0 else 0.0
              O = a * J
              atoms.append({"y": y, "a": a, "O": O})
      return atoms

  def candidate_allocate(packets, z, reservoir_atoms):
      """Returns allocation dict and observed (T_pos, T_neg, D_pos, D_neg) for imbalance diagnostics."""
      D_pos, D_neg, pos_pkts, neg_pkts = compute_drift_demands(packets, z)
      T_pos = sum(a["O"] for a in reservoir_atoms if a["O"] > 0)
      T_neg = sum(-a["O"] for a in reservoir_atoms if a["O"] < 0)
      alloc = {}  # pkt_key -> {"eta_contrib": list of (y, theta* a), "assigned_odd": ...}
      # Positive-drift packets receive from negative-capacity atoms
      for pkt in pos_pkts:
          theta_base = (pkt["D"][z] / D_pos) if D_pos > 0 else 0.0
          contribs = []
          for atom in [aa for aa in reservoir_atoms if aa["O"] < 0]:
              theta = theta_base
              contribs.append((atom["y"], theta * atom["a"], theta))
          alloc[pkt["key"]] = {"neg_from": contribs, "M": pkt["M"]}
      # Negative-drift packets receive from positive-capacity atoms (symmetric)
      for pkt in neg_pkts:
          theta_base = ((-pkt["D"][z]) / D_neg) if D_neg > 0 else 0.0
          contribs = []
          for atom in [aa for aa in reservoir_atoms if aa["O"] > 0]:
              theta = theta_base
              contribs.append((atom["y"], theta * atom["a"], theta))
          alloc[pkt["key"]] = {"pos_from": contribs, "M": pkt["M"]}
      # Zero-drift packets receive nothing from transport (or background)
      return alloc, (T_pos, T_neg, D_pos, D_neg)
  ```

  The resulting η_{p,q,z} for each packet is the signed measure assembled from the weighted atoms above. This preserves exact assembly (thetas per atom sum to 1 on the allocated side) and non-negative weights by construction.

- **Smallest interesting regime to run first:**

  - Regime B (primary MVH): q ≤ 10^5.
  - Regime C (stress, matching existing diagnostic): q ≤ 10^6.
  All runs deterministic; wall time and memory recorded. Python implementation first; C hooks available for larger if needed.

Count and report any accidental negative assignment at |x| < epsilon (must be zero).

Compute the support radius of negative mass per packet and assert it is ≥ M_{p,q}. Also confirm M_{p,q} < 1/2 for all packets in the regime (per strategy documents). Verify this quantity is finite (trivial for finite packets). Report its magnitude relative to the total negative transport capacity T_neg used. The Exact Completion Assembly Theorem is a completion-side statement. The PGS objects (ordered prime-gap chambers, divisor-count field via τ, GWR-selected leftmost minimum-divisor integers, prime-power packets P(p,q) inside each chamber, λ = Λ deconvolved coefficients, odd drift D_{p,q}(z) formed from λ-weighted J_z on the centered log coordinates inside each packet) are the source. The invariants (Divisor Normalization Identity, Leftmost Minimum-Divisor Rule, packetwise support properties under GWR) are already proved on the source side. The remaining unresolved target after packet-drift-weighted-average and completion-localization reductions is the global assembly of packetwise measures η_{p,q,z} from the transport reservoir (pole pair at ±1/2 plus trivial-zero atoms at −2m−1/2) such that the four conditions hold and drift is cancelled exactly. The candidate route is the proportional opposite-sign allocation rule applied after a symmetric trivial-zero split that closes sidewise capacities. The primary obstruction remains the Transport Capacity Balance Identity (sidewise, not merely net). The infinite trivial-zero reservoir case is the dominant open analytic difficulty because raw trivial-zero atoms supply only negative odd capacity, the gamma finite part fixes only the net difference of capacities, and no canonical intrinsic rule for the split parameters α_m(z) has been extracted from current local chamber machinery.

**Narrow high-leverage constructive proposals (all conditional; finite-testable where noted):**
1. The candidate allocation rule with explicit "if" conditions has been synthesized into the living draft document CANDIDATE-CONSTRUCTIVE-ALGORITHM-DRAFT.md (created in this proof-construction folder). The rule is fully explicit once the net finite-part packet balance and nonnegative common deficit Δ(z) ≥ 0 are supplied; the resulting η_{p,q,z} are weighted sums of allocated (possibly split) transport atoms. This is a candidate construction. It can be placed under test on any finite chamber regime once a concrete regularization and α-normalization (e.g., nearest-atom) are chosen for the harness.
2. The master state-separation ledger (this file) and the living draft are now the synthesis points. All future agent input is to be folded here under strict vocabulary.

Proposal for verification surface (to be executed under Numerics): implement the candidate_allocate sketch already recorded by Numerics, using explicit pole + truncated trivial-zero reservoir, run on Regime A (q ≤ 10_000) and Regime B (q ≤ 10^5), report every check with "Candidate construction ... observed on finite set ..." headers only. This directly addresses the user success criterion of accompanying code for verification on large finite sets.

**Proof Architect — Initial Architecture Note (proposed exact section structure for all future contributions to maintain coherence):**
To keep the four-agent team coherent across iterations, every contribution (initial or follow-up) appended to the central LEDGER and every update to the living draft or other artifacts must open with and be organized under the following fixed section structure (using only separation vocabulary in every sentence):

1. **Subagent identity and role** — State name, role definition from charter, subagent ID, and date/time of entry.
2. **Status header** — One-sentence classification using exact phrases: "Candidate construction under test on regime X", "Observed on finite set Y with regularization Z", "Remains fully open", "Strategy note for candidate route W", "Invalidated candidate route V on finite diagnostic surface S", etc. Never use result language.
3. **Role-specific assessment of the current obstruction** — Begin from PGS objects and invariants (chambers, packets, GWR, DNI, drifts D_{p,q}(z), reserves R_{p,q}(z), M_{p,q}). Map forward to the completion-side target (η construction, Transport Capacity Balance Identity, infinite trivial-zero case). Identify precisely which sub-problem is closed, which is candidate, which remains open. Reference exact prior documents by path and title.
4. **Narrow, high-leverage constructive proposals** — At most two or three. For each: state the concrete step, all explicit "if" conditions, the finite regime on which it can be placed under test, the precise open bound that would remain even if the step succeeds on that regime (especially infinite trivial-zero reservoir), and the exact verification checks against the four theorem conditions or the two supporting lemmas (net finite-part balance, Δ ≥ 0).
5. **Contribution to master artifacts** — Explicit list of what was written or edited (e.g., "Appended this entry to LEDGER", "Updated section 3.2 of CANDIDATE-CONSTRUCTIVE-ALGORITHM-DRAFT.md with new table row for X", "Proposed new experiment file path Y"). Include the full absolute path of every new or modified file.
6. **Recommended next direction for the team (or for specific roles)** — One or two sentences using separation vocabulary only. Identify the highest-leverage next narrow step that respects the time-box and user success criterion.
7. **Language audit statement** — "Every sentence in this entry has been reviewed against the separation vocabulary contract. No optimistic language, no downgrading of proved PGS theorems, no classical-first framing, no reframing of deterministic objects as probabilistic."

This structure ensures that the Proof Architect can synthesize without reconstruction and that the ledger remains the single source of truth. The living draft CANDIDATE-CONSTRUCTIVE-ALGORITHM-DRAFT.md will be the sole place where the synthesized candidate algorithm, the sub-problem table, and the open-bounds statement are maintained; ledger entries feed it but do not duplicate its content.

This architecture note itself is a strategy note for maintaining team coherence. It is not a claim of progress.

**All new and edited content in this entry (the draft and this ledger append) has received a final language edit pass by the Proof Architect for maximum strictness of separation vocabulary.** No sentence uses "we have shown", "progress toward", "works", "supports the theorem", or any equivalent. All preconditions, open cases (especially infinite trivial-zero), and finite-only status are stated explicitly.

**End of Proof Architect initial contribution and architecture note.**
